=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
There is an integer sequence A = (A_2,A_3,\ldots,A_N). Also, for an integer sequence P=(P_2, P_3, \ldots ,P_N) where 1 \leq P_i \leq i-1 for each i (2 \leq i \leq N), define the weighted tree T(P) with N vertices, rooted at vertex 1, as follows:

- A rooted tree where, for each i (2 \leq i \leq N), the parent of i is P_i, and the weight of the edge between i and P_i is A_i.

You are given Q queries. Process them in order. The i-th query is as follows:

- You are given integers u_i and v_i, each between 1 and N. For each of the possible (N-1)! sequences P, take the tree T(P) and consider the distance between vertices u_i and v_i in this tree. Output the sum, modulo 998244353, of these distances over all T(P). Here, the distance between two vertices u_i and v_i is the sum of the weights of the edges on the unique path (not visiting the same vertex more than once) that connects them.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N Q
A_2 A_3 \ldots A_N
u_1 v_1
u_2 v_2
\vdots
u_Q v_Q

Output

Print Q lines. The i-th line should contain the answer to the i-th query.

Constraints


- 2 \leq N \leq 2 \times 10^5
- 1 \leq Q \leq 2 \times 10^5
- 1 \leq A_i \leq 10^9
- 1 \leq u_i < v_i \leq N
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

3 2
1 1
1 2
1 3

Sample Output 1

2
3


- If P = (1,1), then in the tree T(P), the distance between vertices 1 and 2 is 1, and the distance between vertices 1 and 3 is 1.
- If P = (1,2), then in the tree T(P), the distance between vertices 1 and 2 is 1, and the distance between vertices 1 and 3 is 2.

Therefore, the total distance between vertices 1 and 2 over all T(P) is 2, and the total distance between vertices 1 and 3 over all T(P) is 3.

Sample Input 2

2 1
100
1 2

Sample Output 2

100

Sample Input 3

9 6
765689282 93267307 563699854 951829154 801512848 389123318 924504746 596035433
3 8
2 5
5 8
2 9
8 9
5 7

Sample Output 3

55973424
496202632
903509579
343265517
550981449
68482696

Remember to take the sum modulo 998244353.

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Alternatively, use conditional on F_{u-1}: I_u ~ Bern(S_{u-1}/(u-1)), and S_u = S_{u-1}+I_u. Then
E[I_u S_u] = E[ E[I_u S_u | F_{u-1}] ] = E[ (S_{u-1}/(u-1))*(S_{u-1}+1) ] = (E[S_{u-1}^2] + E[S_{u-1}])/(u-1).
Need first two moments of S_{u-1}. We can compute for Pólya urn.

For Pólya urn starting with a=1, b=x-1, after n = u-1-x draws? Wait S_{u-1} after vertices up to u-1: initial at x, draws for vertices x+1..u-1, count n = u-1-x. Total at that time = u-1. We know E[S_{u-1}] = (u-1)/x? Because each vertex j>x has prob 1/x in subtree, plus x: 1 + (u-1-x)/x = (u-1)/x. Yes.

Need E[S_{u-1}^2]. For Pólya urn, there is formula. Let's derive.

Pólya urn initial a=1, b=x-1. After n draws, number of A balls S = 1 + K. The expected square maybe:

Let n = u-1-x, T = u-1 = x+n.
E[S] = T/x.
Var = n (x-1) T / (x^2 (x+1)).

= T[(x+1)+(x-1)] - x(x-1) + x(x+1)
= 2xT + [-x^2+x + x^2+x] = 2xT + 2x = 2x(T+1) = 2x u.

Let's write code cleanly:

import sys
MOD = 998244353

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    it = iter(data)
    N = next(it); Q = next(it)
    A = [0]*(N+1)
    for i in range(2, N+1):
        A[i] = next(it) % MOD

    inv = [0]*(N+2)
    inv[1] = 1
    for i in range(2, N+2):
        inv[i] = MOD - (MOD // i) * inv[MOD % i] % MOD

    pref1 = [0]*(N+2)
    pref2 = [0]*(N+2)
    for i in range(2, N+1):
        ai = A[i]
        pref1[i] = (pref1[i-1] + ai * inv[i]) % MOD
        term = ai * 2 % MOD
        term = term * (i-1) % MOD
        term = term * inv[i] % MOD
        term = term * inv[i+1] % MOD
        pref2[i] = (pref2[i-1] + term) % MOD

    fact = 1
    for i in range(2, N):
        fact = fact * i % MOD

    out = []
    for _ in range(Q):
        u = next(it); v = next(it)
        ans = A[v]
        if u >= 2:
            ans = (ans + A[u] * (u-1) % MOD * inv[u]) % MOD
        if u >= 3:  # u-1 >=2
            ans = (ans + pref2[u-1] - pref2[1]) % MOD
        if v - 1 >= u + 1:
            ans = (ans + pref1[v-1] - pref1[u]) % MOD
        ans = ans * fact % MOD
        out.append(str(ans))
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Let's write code:

def main():
    import sys
    MOD=998244353
    data=list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    idx=0
    N=data[idx]; Q=data[idx+1]; idx+=2
    A=[0]*(N+1)
    for i in range(2,N+1):
        A[i]=data[idx]%MOD; idx+=1
    inv=[0]*(N+2)
    inv[1]=1
    for i in range(2,N+2):
        inv[i]=(MOD - (MOD//i)*inv[MOD%i] % MOD) % MOD
    pref1=[0]*(N+2)
    pref2=[0]*(N+2)
    for i in range(2,N+1):
        ai=A[i]
        pref1[i]=(pref1[i-1]+ai*inv[i])%MOD
        term=ai*2%MOD
        term=term*(i-1)%MOD
        term=term*inv[i]%MOD
        term=term*inv[i+1]%MOD
        pref2[i]=(pref2[i-1]+term)%MOD
    fact=1
    for i in range(2,N):
        fact=fact*i%MOD
    out=[]
    for _ in range(Q):
        u=data[idx]; v=data[idx+1]; idx+=2
        ans=A[v]
        if u>=2:
            ans += A[u]*(u-1)%MOD*inv[u]
        if u>=3:
            ans += pref2[u-1] - pref2[1]
        if v >= u+2:
            ans += pref1[v-1] - pref1[u]
        ans %= MOD
        ans = ans * fact % MOD
        out.append(str(ans))
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))

Now, let's revisit the derivation of P(x ancestor of both) using Pólya urn to ensure no off-by-one in denominator x(x+1) vs (x+1)(x+2). Let's test with x=2,u=3,v=4: p=1/3. If formula 2/(x(x+1)) yes. If 2/((x+1)(x+2)) =2/(3*4)=1/6 no. Good.

Now, let's think about if for x<u, P(x ancestor of u)=1/x. Then P(x ancestor of both) = P(x ancestor of u) * P(x ancestor of v | x ancestor of u). Conditional = [2/(x(x+1))] / (1/x) = 2/(x+1). For x=2 conditional=2/3; x=3 conditional=1/2; x=4 conditional=2/5. This is the probability that v is in subtree x given u is in subtree x. In Pólya urn, given u in subtree, the expected proportion S_u/u? Actually conditional probability = E[S_u/u | u in sub] = E[S_u | u in sub]/u. We computed E[S_u | u in sub] = [E[I_u S_u]/P] = [2u/(x(x+1))] / (1/x) = 2u/(x+1). Divide by u =2/(x+1). Nice. So conditional probability =2/(x+1). For x=2, 2/3; x=3,1/2. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with if the code's `pref1` array has index up to N, so pref1[N-1]
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys

MOD = 998244353

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    idx = 0
    N = data[idx]
    Q = data[idx + 1]
    idx += 2

    A = [0] * (N + 1)
    for i in range(2, N + 1):
        A[i] = data[idx] % MOD
        idx += 1

    inv = [0] * (N + 2)
    inv[1] = 1
    for i in range(2, N + 2):
        inv[i] = (MOD - (MOD // i) * inv[MOD % i] % MOD) % MOD

    pref1 = [0] * (N + 2)  # pref1[k] = sum_{i=2..k} A_i / i
    pref2 = [0] * (N + 2)  # pref2[k] = sum_{i=2..k} A_i * 2(i-1) / (i(i+1))

    for i in range(2, N + 1):
        ai = A[i]

        pref1[i] = (pref1[i - 1] + ai * inv[i]) % MOD

        term = ai * 2 % MOD
        term = term * (i - 1) % MOD
        term = term * inv[i] % MOD
        term = term * inv[i + 1] % MOD
        pref2[i] = (pref2[i - 1] + term) % MOD

    fact = 1
    for i in range(2, N):
        fact = fact * i % MOD

    out = []
    for _ in range(Q):
        u = data[idx]
        v = data[idx + 1]
        idx += 2

        ans = A[v]

        if u >= 2:
            ans += A[u] * (u - 1) % MOD * inv[u] % MOD

        if u >= 3:
            ans += pref2[u - 1]

        if v >= u + 2:
            ans += pref1[v - 1] - pref1[u]

        ans %= MOD
        ans = ans * fact % MOD
        out.append(str(ans))

    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
